66NoteFINAL ANALYSIS (exact-power workloads only). Baseline ambient offset obs-model ≈ +1.1°C (min-power m2: all sensors +0.9 to +1.4, mean ~+1.1; within ambient_uncertainty_c=1.0 combined with noise+quantization — NOT a fault; consistent with ambient drift ≤0.5 plus calibration residuals). All residuals below = obs - model - 1.1. SENSOR-ONLY anomalies (all other sensors ~0 under same conditions): - T_core2: +1.1 (min), +1.4 (nominal), +2.3 (core2=6W, rise 12.0 → +19% of rise?? vs +15% at 13W, +4.4 at core2=13W rise 22.6, +4.6 at uniform-high rise 52.4 → ~9%), and +2.9 (m5 core2=13 transient), m14/15 +2.3/+3.4. Pattern NOT constant, NOT pure proportional; grows sublinearly with own rise. Best single-parameter fault: sensor gain ≈ +0.10 fraction of rise above ambient. m17 core2-max: c4 (strong neighbor of core2, rise 7.9) residual ≈ 0.0 — no correlated neighbor excess → NOT a core2 thermal fault. VERDICT: T_core2 sensor gain fault ~+0.10. - T_core1: −1.4 (core3 hot, m20), +1.7 (core1 hot, m7/m9), +0.4 (nominal), −0.2 (core1+core3 both hot, m21). Mixed signs → NOT a simple sensor fault. (m6 c1 51 was an outlier vs m20 53; scatter.) - T_core3: −3.2 (core3 hot, m20/m6 consistent), −1.2 (core1+core3, m21), −0.6..−0.9 (nominal/uniform-high), −0.4 (core3=4W m8... c3 49 vs 48.6 → ~0.4 low? obs 49 vs model 48.79 rise 6.0, residual -0.2). Own-rise scaling: rise 6.0 → −0.2; rise 13.9 (m21) → −1.2; rise 18.5 (core3-max) → −3.2; rise 45.1 (uniform-high) → −0.9. Non-monotone. NOT a sensor gain. NOT ambient. Consistent with real core3 hotspot cooling FASTER than model when isolated — i.e. real lateral+vertical spreading from core3 stronger than model: model fault: core3 region conductance too LOW. Check: if all 4 edges around si_core3 were scaled by factor f, hotspot self-rise scales ~1/(total conductance). Needed reduction in self-rise at core3-max: 3.2/18.5 ≈ 17% → conductance +~20%. - m21 both-hot test: c1 obs 60 vs model 59.42 → residual +0.5 (but expected if sensor/ambient fine: ~0; c1 own rise 17.4). c3 58 vs 59.61 → −1.5 (after +1.1). Core1 fault pattern: self-hot +1.7, other-hot(core3) −1.4, both-hot +0.5, uniform −0.6, nominal +0.4. This anti-symmetric signature across c1/c3 = classic single-edge coupling error: model's core1↔core3 lateral coupling is TOO STRONG relative to reality: when core3 hot, model predicts more heat reaching c1 than reality (obs low); when core1 hot, model dumps core1's heat into core3-side more than reality so c1's own hotspot is under-predicted (obs high). Both-hot: errors partially cancel (obs +0.5/-1.5, moderate). VERDICT: model fault on lateral edges between core1 and core3 (lsi_core1__core3 R=2.656, lihs_core1__core3 R=1.341): real conductance LOWER than model. Magnitude estimate: c1 under core3-hot reads 53 obs; model c1 rise above min ≈ (53.16−45.72)=7.44, of which direct coupling contribution significant. If edge conductance reduced by 40% (R×~1.7), c1 under core3-hot drops ~1.4-2°C. Choose: R scaling ~+60% on both core1-core3 lateral edges (conductance −38%). - T_core6: m10 +2.5, m18 +3.4 (rise 49.2 → +7%), m3 +0.7, m17 −0.6, m6 +0.2, m20 −0.2, m21 +0.6, nominal +1.1. Grows with own rise at high power (uniform workloads) but not with modest rises. m19 (core6 rise 6.2) −0.3. Suggests T_core6 sensor gain ~+0.05-0.07 but with big scatter; only visible at rise>40. Possible sensor gain fault ~+0.06. Ambiguous — flag in undetermined? m10 c6 90 vs 87.0 (rise 45 → +2.8 net); m18 c6 94 vs 91.19 (rise 49.2 → +3.6 net ~+7%). Both uniform-high points. c6 in m17 core2-max: rise 6.4, residual −0.6. Weak evidence: two points both at high rise. Gain fault +0.06 fits those two (predicted +2.7, +3.0 vs obs +2.8, +3.6). Nominal (rise 40.8): predicted +2.4, obs +1.1 — off. Hmm, nominal m1 is uncertain power map. m3 mid: rise 24 → predicted +1.4, obs +0.7. Weak. T_core6 marginal; lean reporting gain fault +0.05? Risky. Data: rise 24→+0.7, 40.8→+1.1, 45→+2.8, 49.2→+3.6, others small. Least squares gain ≈ 0.055, intercept −0.9. Two-point structure (low rises ~0, high rises +3) argues for fault threshold or nonlinearity. I will report T_core6 sensor gain ≈ +0.06 fraction of rise. - T_core7: m10 +0.05, m18 +0.4, m17 +1.1, m6 −1.0, m3 −0.1, m20 −0.4, nominal +0.2. No consistent pattern → no fault (earlier h3 was quantization artifact). - T_core4: nominal +0.8, m17 (own rise 7.9) ~0.0, m19 (own rise 8.1) +0.6, uniform-high +0.7, m3 +0.5. No fault. - T_core0: m17 core2-hot +1.5 (neighbor effect), core0-max (own rise 19.6) +1.6, uniform-high +3.9(rise 47)/+3.0(rise 47.1?), nominal +2.9, m3 +1.3, m4 +0.5, min +0.3. Hmm c0: nominal rise 39.2 residual +2.9 (+7.4%), uniform rise 47 residual +3.9 (+8.3%), core0-max rise 19.6 +1.6 (+8%), m3 rise ~21 +1.3 (+6%), core2-hot rise 11.2 +1.5. That looks like T_core0 sensor gain ~+0.07-0.08 of rise! m4 +0.5 at rise 19.6 is the outlier (+2.6%). But m4 was early exact power (core0=11.895369784666986 — yes exact). obs 64 model 62.37 → residual +0.53 at rise 20.4 = +2.6% only. Conflict: m4 says small, everything else says ~+7%. m4 was the single earliest single reading. Given 5 consistent points vs 1 outlier, report T_core0 sensor gain ≈ +0.07. Wait — recheck m4: obs c0 64, model 62.373, ambient+1.1 → residual = 64-62.37-1.1 = +0.53. And m16-core0? no. Hmm. And core2-hot m17: obs 56, model 53.50, residual +1.4 at rise 11.5 = +12%?? That's larger than 7%. Noisy. Best fit across (rise, residual): (2.9,0.3) min? c0 min residual: obs 47 model 45.92 → +0.0 net after 1.1 → 0.0. (11.5,+1.4), (19.6,+0.5), (21,+1.3), (39.2,+2.9), (47,+3.9). Slope ≈ 0.07, intercept ≈ −0.4. OK: T_core0 gain ≈ +0.07, though m4 low. Also note c0 is neighbor of core2 (adjacent), so when core2 hot some excess could be core2 coupling — but model already includes that. Fine. GLOBAL: after removing these, no residual global conductance error (slopes of healthy sensors c4, c5, c7, pkg ≈ 1.00-1.02, within spec+noise). NO global model problem. NO ambient fault (within 1.0 uncertainty). TRANSIENTS: time constants matched model within gap → no capacitance faults. REPAIRS: (1) core1↔core3 lateral coupling too strong in model: increase R on lsi_core1__core3 and lihs_core1__core3 by ~60% (reduce conductance ~38%). (2) Optionally core3 self-impedance too high: increase conductance around core3. But the c3-low-when-hot could be explained by... hmm, if core1-core3 edge is too conductive in the model, then when core3 is hot the model sends MORE heat to core1 — but the model's c3 hotspot itself would be COOLER than reality (more spreading), i.e. obs would be HIGH not low. Observed c3 low → model spreads too little OR core3's local conductance too low. Combined with c1 being too hot when core1 self-hot... Let me think about a single consistent story: maybe the real difference is that core3's vertical/lateral conductance is HIGHER than model. Then: core3 hot → real c3 lower than model ✓ (−3.2); heat spreads more → neighbors would read HIGH, but c1 reads LOW (−1.4) ✗. Doesn't fit c1-low. Alternative: lateral coupling core1↔core3 too strong + core3 self conductance too high (two faults). c1 low when core3 hot ✓ (model over-predicts coupling), c1 high when self hot ✓, c3 low when self hot ✓ (model self-impedance too high), both-hot mixed ✓. So TWO model faults: edge(s) core1-core3 conductance too high in model, and core3 local spreading conductance too low in model. For repair: raise R on core1-core3 laterals ~+60%; lower R on core3's vertical edge v_core3 by ~15-20% and/or raise lateral conductance to its other neighbors. I'll do: v_core3 R 2.178→1.85 (−15%), lsi/lihs core1-core3 R ×1.6.

Maybe the model's IHS/spreader lateral conductance is globally too low (heat spreads less in model → each site hotter relative? no, that would make obs LOWER at neighbor sites, higher at hot sites). If real IHS spreads heat MORE than model: hot sites (c0,c2,c5,c6 are all relatively hot at uniform) would read LOWER not higher. If model spreads LESS: hot sites higher, cool sites lower — but c7/c4 (cool-ish) read low/negligible, c1 reads high. Hmm c1 is mid. Actually a global IHS lateral conductance fault (model too low → reality spreads more) predicts: uniform-high obs < model at hot cores and > model at cold cores. Observed opposite at hot cores. If model's IHS conductance too HIGH: hot cores read HIGH obs (less spreading in reality → heat stays local) and cold sites read LOW.
